That was why he was even more worried. Because even with all of them there, they had not been able to kill the Heavenly Slaughter Star.

Geom Muyang tried to shake off his anxiety and made a joke.

“It must be because Mugeuk is talking too much and delaying the fight.”

He heard Hwi laugh quietly.

It was right then.

Flash!

Watching the whole world brighten in the distance, Geom Muyang was startled.

Hwi, who had revealed himself before he knew it, shouted beside Geom Muyang.

“Heavenly Demon Destroys the World has been unleashed.”

Geom Muyang’s expression hardened at once. A battle had taken place that required even Heavenly Demon Destroys the World, the final technique of the Nine Flowers Demonic Art.

When the light disappeared, Geom Muyang said to Hwi,

“Put out the Demon Flower and come with me.”

Hwi looked at him in surprise.

“You cannot.”

In a situation where the Cult Leader’s and Young Cult Leader’s lives were unknown, he could not send out the Cult Leader’s one remaining blood relative.

“In any case, now that Heavenly Demon Destroys the World has been unleashed, the battle should be over.”

If the worst-case scenario had unfolded.

“If we lost, could these mechanisms and formations stop him?”

Hwi agreed with his words.

The two immediately shot off and left the main cult.

When they arrived at Maga Village, there was nothing there.

It was truly a space of nothingness, where everything had vanished.

And then an astonishing sight entered their eyes.

A single table.

There was a single table there.

The thing Geom Mugeuk had looked at earlier and said, You survived too, was that table.

Everything had vanished under Hwa Mugi’s Heavenly Demon Destroys the World, but the table and chairs that had happened to be behind the Great Demon Wall in the Pungryu Tavern had remained intact.

It was not as if they had deliberately tried to protect it, but by chance, that was what happened. And it was the very table where they had eaten together.

Geom Mugeuk walked over and stroked the table.

Could it be that the heavens were speaking to him? Telling him to keep living while protecting his demonic path in the future as well.

When Geom Muyang and Hwi arrived, everyone was sitting at that table.

With Geom Ujin at the center, Jin Paecheon and Baek Jagang sat on either side of him. Beside them sat Kwonma and the Poison King, and at the end sat Geom Mugeuk, Cult Leader Pungcheon, and the Sword King.

Sitting like that in a space where nothing else existed, they looked like a scene from a painting.

“Hyung! Over here! Uncle Hwi! Welcome!”

Seeing Geom Mugeuk’s bright appearance, Geom Muyang’s tension eased. No one would know with what feelings he had come all the way here.

Geom Muyang walked quickly toward his father.

Hwi, who had been walking with him, vanished soundlessly. It was the moment he entered into his original duty.

Geom Muyang came forward and greeted Geom Ujin respectfully. He said nothing else.

“I greet the Cult Leader. Congratulations on repelling the Heavenly Slaughter Star.”

Geom Ujin nodded and gave the credit to the two Alliance Leaders.

“We won safely because the two of them helped us.”

Geom Muyang expressed his gratitude to the two Alliance Leaders.

After finishing his greetings even to Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Demon Venerables, Geom Mugeuk pulled Geom Muyang into the seat beside him.

“I missed you, Hyung.”

Had he truly missed him as much as he himself had? For the first time, he had prayed to the heavens. That everyone would be safe, that his younger brother would welcome him with his usual banter.

Geom Muyang looked around and said,

“We will have to rebuild starting from here.”

“We should build them a splendid new building. Bigger and taller. In fact, let’s just tell everyone to take a good rest this time.”

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk. With eyes gentler than ever before,

“You look tired.”

It was the first time. The first time his brother had looked at him and told him he looked tired.

Geom Mugeuk leaned back crookedly in his chair and stretched his legs out.

“Tomorrow, I’m going to sleep all day. No, wait. On a day like this, even if I’m tired, I’ll be too happy to sleep.”

Just then, more people arrived there.

The three who came running like the wind and landed were the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, One Flower Sword Venerable, and Extreme Evil Soma.

Those who had set out first in the first group had arrived first.

Because of their injuries, Demon Buddha, the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable, and the Drunken Demon were returning by carriage.

The three Demon Venerables could tell from the sight of Maga Village just how difficult and tremendous this battle had been.

Geom Mugeuk shouted to them.

“Elder! Sword Venerable! Soma!”

Geom Mugeuk sprang to his feet and ran over like a child, delighted to see them. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon avoided Geom Mugeuk, who was about to fling himself into his arms, with footwork and walked toward Geom Ujin.

One Flower Sword Venerable walked after him and said to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon,

“I told you, didn’t I? The Young Cult Leader went ahead first, so it would be fine.”

One Flower Sword Venerable spoke to Geom Mugeuk as if tattling.

“You cannot imagine how much he urged us to hurry.”

Geom Mugeuk shouted at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s back.

“I want to hurry to your house, Elder, pile books all over the bed, and read while lying down!”

“Is that your bed?”

Extreme Evil Soma, who came walking last, smiled brightly beneath his mask.

Geom Mugeuk smiled back. As always, that was enough for their greeting upon reunion.

The three Demon Venerables greeted Geom Ujin, and also greeted the Alliance Leaders.

The two Alliance Leaders felt a different aura from the three Demon Venerables than before.

Not only Geom Mugeuk, but even the Demon Venerables—they were growing every time they met.

After the greetings were over, One Flower Sword Venerable said to Geom Ujin. Because everyone was safe, he could make such a joke.

“We should have finished touring the Central Plains before coming back.”

Geom Ujin asked with a smile,

“I hear you went to many places?”

“We went to Taihu as well. Do you perhaps remember it?”

Geom Ujin nodded as if he did.

“When I went there, it had not changed as much as I thought.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been listening, said to his father,

“Since it came up, the four of you should go again.”

At that, Jin Paecheon, who had been listening, said with a smile,

“When you do, be sure to send word to us. Otherwise, there will be another emergency.”

Jin Paecheon and Baek Jagang rose from their seats.

“We will be going now.”

They were truly people who came like the wind and were about to leave like the wind.

Geom Ujin likewise did not forcibly hold them back. The fact that they had come all the way here was already more than enough to be grateful for, and it was also something that must not become known to the outside.

Geom Ujin respectfully cupped his fist to the two of them in farewell.

“I will not forget the help you gave this time.”

After they had finished exchanging farewells, Baek Jagang spoke last.

“Thank you for teaching me to cook.”

“I still have some dishes I’ve been saving.”

“I hope I’ll have another chance to learn.”

After exchanging those words, Geom Ujin turned and walked away first. His elder brother and the five Demon Venerables followed behind him, and Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Sword King walked after them.

The two had gone in as well so that Geom Mugeuk could see the two alliance leaders off at ease.

Even though not all the Demon Venerables had gathered, the sight of them walking away looked so majestic it was as if they had spread their wings.

As Jin Paecheon watched their retreating backs, his gaze deepened. Throughout this fight, he had been more worried about Geom Ujin and the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult than about Hwa Mugi.

Geom Ujin was strong. Far too strong.

He recalled the Heavenly Demon Annihilates the World that Geom Ujin had unleashed earlier.

A sword of light that had flown through the light.

Of course, he had said that using Heavenly Demon Annihilates the World would exhaust almost all of one’s internal energy, but Jin Paecheon was not confident he could block that attack.

In short, if he fought Geom Ujin, he would die.

And on top of that, there was the Heavenly Demon Soul.

‘No one in the present-day murim can defeat Cult Leader Geom.’

And was that all? The Demon Venerables were strong as well. The Demon Venerables of this generation were stronger than any in history.

Like Jin Paecheon, Baek Jagang also sensed that Geom Ujin would not abandon his dream of unifying the murim.

If he himself possessed such power, he would never give up. Suddenly, he recalled the words Ju Gasin had left behind.

‘You told me to unite the Evil Path? You gave me far too difficult a mission.’

Geom Mugeuk greeted Jin Paecheon.

“I’ll come visit soon to see Hagun and Haryeong.”

Jin Paecheon looked at Geom Mugeuk and smiled gently. He felt that the fate of the murim would depend on this Young Cult Leader from now on.

But he did not show any sign of placing that burden on him.

“I’ll be waiting.”

He also exchanged farewells with Baek Jagang.

“I’ll come by to have a meal cooked by you personally.”

“I’ll make it spicy and stimulating.”

His father was walking toward the main headquarters, while the two alliance leaders were heading in the opposite direction.

Geom Mugeuk stood in the middle.

Though they had exchanged farewells casually, he could sense that the alliance leaders were tense.

Why are you all being like this? You can’t let things get this taut already!

There’s still plenty of time left before the day I promised I’d persuade Father!

Geom Mugeuk infused his voice with internal energy so everyone could hear him and shouted loudly.

“Starting now, I’m going to go around having fun. If another mastermind appears in the murim, contact Father or those two alliance leaders over there. Conspiracies, schemes, underhanded tricks, stratagems—those words no longer exist for me.

From now on, all I’ll have are things like rest, hanging out with friends, travel, cooking, drinking parties, dancing, and reading! Don’t scold me for playing around too much.

You’re playing again? If you keep fooling around like that, who’s going to protect the murim? If you nag me like that, I’m leaving home.”

Chapter 843: Has It Been Decided What Kind of Life You'll Live?
---------------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk entered the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“Welcome, Young Cult Master!”

The martial artists guarding the main gate bowed their heads in unison. Until just moments ago, they had been in the grip of suffocating tension.

When the Demon Flame had first risen, they had not even been able to stand out here. Every mechanism installed at the main gate had been activated, and they had waited at their designated posts.

But when the Demon Flame went out, and Geom Ujin and the Demon Venerables returned safely, they knew that this battle had ended in victory.

The past few days, when the Demon Flame had risen, would be spoken of among their juniors for a long time to come. Perhaps, with exaggeration piled upon exaggeration, it might even be passed down in the end as an adventure tale beginning, “Back on the day the main gate was breached…”

Geom Mugeuk did not return to his residence, but headed to the temporary lodgings where the residents of Maga Village were staying.

“It’s the Young Cult Master!”

“The Young Cult Master has come!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s appearance, the residents of Maga Village rejoiced greatly. Though they had been told that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had brought them here for their own sake, they had all been anxious after being dragged here so suddenly.

And now, the Young Cult Master whom they trusted and followed most had returned.

The residents of Maga Village gathered around the Young Cult Master. Geom Mugeuk looked over the temporary quarters where they had been staying. No matter how much care had been taken, how comfortable could temporary lodgings truly be?

“You have all suffered much.”

Jo Chunbae came running out from among them.

“Young Cult Master! You are safe.”

“Innkeeper, have you been well?”

Geom Mugeuk took Jo Chunbae’s hand.

“Oh dear, how can someone as esteemed as you do this?”

For the Young Cult Master to take his hand in front of so many people was truly an immense honor and joy.

“The fighting outside is all over.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, everyone cheered and rejoiced.

“Does that mean we can go home now?”

“That is true, but there is a problem.”

To the puzzled crowd, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Everyone, follow me.”

Geom Mugeuk led them all out of the headquarters.

When they arrived at Maga Village, the residents stood there in a daze.

Where their homes had once stood, there was nothing. How could not even the collapsed ruins remain? Even seeing it with their own eyes, they could not believe it.

Geom Mugeuk said to them,

“A great battle took place here, so there was no avoiding it.”

Everyone was so stunned that they could not speak. A sigh burst from someone’s mouth, and with that as the start, everyone began to reveal their emotions.

Some were utterly devastated, while others clutched their heads in anguish. Some sank to the ground and burst into tears, and there were also those who could not hide the resentment in their eyes as they looked at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk silently watched as each of them let their emotions spill out.

“How are we supposed to live now?”

“Young Cult Master! Please save us!”

“This is too much!”

The shock of losing their entire foundation made them forget that the person before them was the Young Cult Master.

At that moment, Jo Chunbae shouted,

“You people! What rudeness is this toward the Young Cult Master? If we had been here, we would have vanished just like this.”

At those words, everyone came to their senses. The disappearance of their homes was not the issue. It occurred to them that if Geom Ujin had not taken them away in the first place, they would all be dead.

Jo Chunbae said to Geom Mugeuk,

“Thank you truly for saving us.”

Jo Chunbae showed them that expressing gratitude first was the proper thing to do. Following him, the residents of Maga Village also conveyed their thanks to Geom Mugeuk.

“Thank you, Young Cult Master.”

Yes, even if they resented everyone else, they must not resent the Young Cult Master. They all knew well just how much he had done for them.

Only then did Geom Mugeuk speak to them.

“Our cult will step forward and rebuild your homes.”

Only then did everyone feel relieved. If the Divine Cult did not help them, then from this very day onward, they would truly have no way to live.

That was not the only good news.

“Not only your homes, but we also intend to restore as much as possible of the furnishings and belongings that disappeared inside. We will also provide money so that you can get by for a while.”

Then one of the Maga Village residents who had resented him earlier spoke.

“If you had told us from the beginning, we would not have shown such an ugly sight. I apologize, Young Cult Master.”

With a gentle expression, Geom Mugeuk said to him,

“I did not think of that as an ugly sight.”

There was a reason Geom Mugeuk had not told them that first.

“Even if we build better buildings here and furnish the interiors, we cannot recreate the memories you have accumulated here over decades, can we?”

At those words, not only Jo Chunbae but all the residents of Maga Village were startled. They had truly never imagined that the Young Cult Master would take even such things into consideration.

“So you may grieve more and resent more. If you force yourselves to endure it, you will grow sick with resentment. So be as angry as you want now. Resent as much as you need.”

Several people burst into the tears they had been holding back. Yes, how could this matter be resolved entirely with money? Taller and better buildings were good, but there were things that had to be cared for before that.

“I am sorry for blowing away your memories.”

Representing the residents of Maga Village, Jo Chunbae said,

“No. Just saving us like this and rebuilding our homes is already more grace than we deserve. Thank you, Young Cult Master.”

Following Jo Chunbae, all the other residents also bowed their heads to the Young Cult Master.

“I am grateful that you say so. Starting tomorrow, I will mobilize all the manpower of our cult to construct a new Maga Village. Let us discuss things together as we build your new home.”

If the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult set its mind to it, rebuilding Maga Village would not take long.

“Now, you have all suffered greatly, so think of this as life granting you a vacation and rest well! Ah, and that money is all my father’s money. Thank my father, not me.”

At those words, laughter broke out here and there. The faces of the Maga Village residents brightened fully. The gazes they directed toward Geom Mugeuk were filled with indescribable gratitude and loyalty.

Suddenly, Jo Chunbae recalled something the Young Cult Master had once said. Back then, saying he would absolutely not allow outside pressure, he had declared,

“If someone threatens you by saying they’ll call their father, like that bastard earlier, then I’ll call my father too!”

He was mentioning the Cult Leader just as he had then, but the Young Cult Master of that time and the Young Cult Master now felt different.

‘The Young Cult Master is truly growing into a splendid man.’

Geom Mugeuk walked over beside Jo Chunbae.

“Innkeeper, even so, that one managed to survive.”

In the direction Geom Mugeuk was looking, a single table stood all alone.

“A good idea has come to me. Since it is a historic table, make people pay double if they want to sit there and eat.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Jo Chunbae laughed. He intended to set this table aside and take it home. Then he would pass it down as a family heirloom.

“Do not be too sorry that the writing on the second-floor wall is gone. Once the building is rebuilt, I will invite all those people and have them write new words.”

“No, please do not.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s consolation, Jo Chunbae revealed his honest feelings.

“To be honest, I feel relieved. I said before that I did not care about those words, but every day I was anxious that someone might steal them or that a drunkard might scribble over them. This is actually refreshing.”

Geom Mugeuk said with a laugh,

“That is right. There is no need to show it through writing. You experienced it yourself, did you not? Tell the customers about that day in your own words, one by one. You yourself are the legend, Innkeeper.”

Jo Chunbae looked at Geom Mugeuk with trembling eyes. The Young Cult Master always made him feel this way.

What am I, for him to do this?

Here stood a Young Cult Master who made an old tavern owner into a legend.

Geom Mugeuk sat down at the table and said to Jo Chunbae,

“Innkeeper, bring me some wine.”

He was saying it in jest, knowing full well that everything had disappeared and there was nothing to bring.

It was right then.

Tak.

“Here is your wine.”

Geom Mugeuk was so startled that he sprang to his feet. Someone he truly never could have imagined was standing behind him. It was the old man who had sent him back to the past.

“Elder.”

At some point, everyone around them had stopped. Jo Chunbae had stopped, and the people of Maga Village who had come with him had all stopped moving as if time itself had frozen.

“When we met the second time, you were abstaining from alcohol, so we could not drink, could we? It should be fine now, yes?”

The old man smiled and sat at the table.

This was his third meeting with the old man. When he had met the old man for the second time in the Valley of Wind, the old man had patted his shoulder and said,

“You have suffered much, haven’t you? You are doing well.”

He could not say just how much strength those words had given him.

Geom Mugeuk picked up the wine bottle and filled the old man’s cup.

“Please accept a cup from me.”

Geom Mugeuk filled the old man’s cup to the brim.

This time, the old man filled Geom Mugeuk’s cup.

“At last, you have achieved your long-cherished wish. Killing those who had to be killed, and saving those who had to be saved.”

“It is thanks to you, Elder.”

“Not everyone who returns to the past can live a life like yours. This was not thanks to me. It was something you accomplished.”

The two clinked their cups together and emptied their wine. The wine he had given him was no ordinary wine.

A wondrous change brought about by a single cup of wine.

At that moment, several streams of precelestial true qi and inner energy were mixed within Geom Mugeuk’s body. There was the inner energy based on Hyeok Docheon’s demonic art, the orthodox inner energy of the Martial Emperor Divine Sword, Ju Gasin’s unorthodox inner energy, and even the unique inner energy of the Lord of Cheonui Palace. Not only that, but precelestial true qi of different natures had also been mixed together.

Because Geom Mugeuk possessed vast and pure inner energy, ordinary new inner energy could be converted into his own pure inner energy.

This time, however, the amount of inner energy was enormous, and precelestial true qi of different properties was mixed in with it as well, so melting it all into one was no easy task.

Though the quantity was vast, its purity had fallen, and he had been thinking that he would have no choice but to discard a considerable portion of it.

Sssssss.

But then all of those energies began to merge into one. Inner energy as inner energy, and precelestial true qi as precelestial true qi.

And then they became Geom Mugeuk’s original pure precelestial true qi and inner energy, merging into a single whole. As if they had been Geom Mugeuk’s from the very beginning, they became one.

When that tremendous inner energy became one, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze grew even clearer and deeper. A power that truly surpassed human limits filled Geom Mugeuk’s body to overflowing.

“I thought you would take this power back.”

A moment ago, when those energies had merged into one, he had thought the old man must be about to take them away.

Instead, the old man had made them identical to his own energy, allowing them to become an even greater power.

“It is power you gained through your own efforts. How could I do such a thing?”

Geom Mugeuk had guessed one fact.

“You are the one who arranged the Six Paths Primordial Qi throughout the murim, are you not?”

The old man merely smiled an inscrutable smile, as if to say that such things could not be revealed, but Geom Mugeuk knew. It had surely been arranged by this old man.

Just as he had gone through the process of finding the materials for the Great Art of Regression, after returning he had gone through the trials of obtaining those six energies.

The old man looked at Geom Mugeuk with gentle eyes and said,

“You have worked hard.”

He was the only person who knew how much Geom Mugeuk had suffered in his life before the regression.

“Do you remember? The answer you gave when I asked what sort of life you wanted to live after you finished your revenge.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. He remembered it as vividly as if it had happened only a few days ago.

“I do not know either. Whether I will become the Heavenly Demon after my father, live quietly with my back turned to the world, become a libertine who takes all the beauties of the world, leave the cult and live a life beating the trash of the world, or perhaps become trash myself… I have decided nothing.”

The old man asked,

“Have you decided now? What kind of life you will live?”

After thinking for a moment, Geom Mugeuk calmly answered,

“I still have not found a life I can answer in a single phrase. Whether I will become the Heavenly Demon or a free spirit roaming the murim. But whatever life I live, I will keep just one thing. To live as joyfully as possible in each moment.”

Then, as if he had remembered something important, Geom Mugeuk asked in a complaining tone,

“No, and should you not be asking this question after I persuade my father, who is trying to unite the murim? I now have something more difficult left than catching Hwa Mugi! You know, don’t you? What kind of person my father is.”

The old man seemed to find the situation amusing instead.

“Is life not a succession of hardships?”

“Surely you are not saying there will be another hardship after that?”

“How can one know the affairs of the world?”

Indeed, aside from the matter of his father, would there be anything that could not be resolved with his status and his martial arts?

As if he had seen straight into Geom Mugeuk’s thoughts, the old man said,

“Your child might cause you trouble.”

A child! Geom Mugeuk stared at the old man in astonishment.

“Surely not? You are not preparing such a thing and getting ready to enjoy it, are you?”

“How could that go according to my will? It depends on your heart.”

“Please do not say such frightening things.”

A child? It was a matter he had never once thought about, but as his relationship with his father deepened, there had been times when he had thought of children.

Out of a desire to become a father like his own father.

“I will never be able to become such a good father as my father. I would need to be able to suppress my child with just a glance like he does, but I talk too much, so am I not already destined to be a nagging father?”

“You could also become an ideal father and son who talk a great deal with each other.”

Geom Mugeuk stared intently at the old man and shook his head.

“Do not try to steer me in some strange direction for no reason. I am not getting married!”

The old man, looking at Geom Mugeuk with a pleased smile, bid him farewell.

“I have had wine with you, so I should be going now.”

Geom Mugeuk felt regret at parting with the old man. This meeting was different from their first, and different from their second as well.

“You asked me how I would live, but my life from now on may disappoint you. I have already used up almost all the coolness I am capable of.”

“There is no need to worry. The people around you, whom you have made splendid, will lead you to become even more splendid.”

Looking at the old man, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly.

“Until now, I was too preoccupied to properly greet you.”

Geom Mugeuk performed a deep bow to the old man. With all his heart, he conveyed his gratitude to the heavens.

“Thank you truly for sending me back.”

As the old man slowly disappeared, he left behind his final words.

“Watching over your life has been a great joy to me.”

And with that, the old man vanished.

Geom Mugeuk looked up at the sky and said,

“Please continue watching over me from now on.”

At that moment, Jo Chunbae, who had begun moving again at some point behind him, spoke. He naturally thought Geom Mugeuk’s request to watch over him had been directed at him.

“Whether I watch or not, Young Cult Master, you will become the greatest Cult Leader in the history of the murim.”

Chapter 844: I Can't Give Up Because of You
-------------------------------------------
“You said you were going to play yourself to death from now on, so what are you doing here?”

Still keeping his eyes on his book, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk was seated across from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, diligently writing something on a sheet of paper.

“I’m organizing the things I need to do from here on.”

“What could there be so much of?”

The paper was packed with tasks. They were things he had put off, telling himself he would do them once they caught Hwa Mugi.

First, there were a great many people he had to meet.

He wanted to meet Jin Hagun and Bi Sain and drink a celebratory cup with them. Though the final finishing blow had been delivered by their cult, those two had been part of that long process as well. If not for his ties with them, there would have been no reason for the Murim Alliance Leader and the Sado Alliance Leader to come help them. Ah, he also wanted to see Jin Haryeong and Han Seol of the Ice Palace.

He wanted to have a drink with Seo Daeryong and Jang Ho after so long, and he intended to drink with the Sword King and Cha Iran too.

He had to meet the Demon Venerables as well. First, he wanted to speak separately with the Extreme Evil Young Demon, and when the Drunken Demon arrived at the main headquarters, he wanted to drink with him.

He also had to look after the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, who had fought splendidly as the youngest, and he wanted to visit the Poison King and talk about this battle. Since he had done nothing, he might actually have the most to say.

“Who else do I need to see?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon, who had been reading, glanced sidelong at what was written on the paper.

“What’s that there? The Four Handsome Men’s Expedition to the Central Plains?”

It was the most eye-catching line among everything written there.

“Ah, the representative handsome men of our cult are going out to play in the Central Plains together. This is a plan I made a long time ago.”

“And who are those four?”

Curiosity flickered across the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s face.

“Naturally, I’m the first. Next is the Young Demon, then the Poison King, and finally the Drunken Demon. The four of us. In truth, there was a time when I lost to Father in popularity, but that was only because Daeryong’s sister-in-law has unique tastes.”

What the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was curious about was this.

“Is that masked fellow really that good-looking?”

“It’s no joke.”

The emotion contained in the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s slightly displeased expression was exactly this:

The masked fellow, the drunkard, and the poison freak are included among the four, but I’m left out?

In the end, unable to hold back, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked indirectly.

“What about me?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Blade Demon and smiled.

“If we go by your firm, taut skin right now, then of course you would come right after me, Elder. But if we set out as five, would Sword Venerable be all right with that?”

Of course she would not be. As the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned his gaze back to his book, Geom Mugeuk asked with a smile.

“How was the trip?”

“Not bad.”

That was what he said, but it had been the most enjoyable trip of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s life. He had seen many places he wondered if he would ever be able to visit again. Most of all, he had loved the fact that he was with Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

So much so that there had been many moments when he could not quite get used to her treating him with warmth.

The only regret was the thought of how wonderful it would have been if they had come when they were a little younger.

“Travel diligently while you’re young.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk began writing something again.

A trip with Cult Leader Pungcheon and Gowol, a trip alone with my brother, a trip with Ian. And traveling with Father.

“Why are you taking such care of the men? Go have fun with Ian.”

If he asked her to go away together, Ian would be truly delighted. She was a woman who had followed him like a shadow all her life, silently guarding his side.

If she met another man, she would surely put all nine of her tails to use and show a far more alluring side of herself, yet before him, the Young Cult Leader, she was always busy hiding those tails.

“Elder, have you never thought about getting married?”

At the sudden question, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s hand paused for a moment as he turned a page.

“What marriage at my age?”

“What does age matter?”

“People would point fingers.”

“I’ll cut off every one of those fingers.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon lifted his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“And what about you?”

“Me? Marriage at my age? It’s still too early. My brother hasn’t married either.”

“Do you have any intention of doing so?”

Geom Mugeuk turned his head and looked out the window. Tonight, the moon was especially full and bright.

“I don’t know if I can make someone happy.”

“What do you mean, make someone happy? Marriage isn’t keeping the other person from getting rained on. It’s being willing to stand in the rain together.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s gaze returned to his book.

Looking at him, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Perhaps Sword Venerable is waiting to stand in the rain with you too, Elder.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon laughed self-mockingly.

“We’re… just pitiful.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon for a moment, then began writing again.

Under Marry off my brother and Marry off Ak Gunhak, he wrote in very small letters: Elder too, without fail.

And were those the only things he had to do?

He still had to bring the Nine Flames Demonic Art, which had surpassed the tenth level and reached the eleventh during this battle, to Great Completion at the twelfth level. This was something he had to accomplish in the shortest possible time through the Heaven-Defying Secret Art.

And he wanted to laze around alone all day inside the Space-Time Transposition Art. One day on an island beneath a blazing sun, another in a hot spring amid snowy scenery.

As he watched Geom Mugeuk write diligently, a faint smile formed on the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s lips.

Whatever else might be said, he was grateful that Geom Mugeuk had come to him first. Even when the boy was wondering what to do, he had come here to do it. There was a certain pride in being the Young Cult Leader’s first.

While writing down what he needed to do, Geom Mugeuk walked over to the window. Standing there for a moment, looking up at the brilliantly bright moon, he calmly called to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Elder.”

“What?”

“This is something I truly want to bury deep and take out only much later, but I feel that when it comes to this, you would know best.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon raised his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Do you think Father will really try to unify murim? He knows I’m dead set against it, and he’s become closer than before with the two Alliance Leaders. Do you think he’ll still do it?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon sank into thought for a moment, then at last opened his mouth in a calm tone.

“The unification of murim…”

After a brief pause, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon added in a firm voice.

“He will push ahead with it.”

There was not the slightest hint of jest in the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s expression. It meant he truly saw it that way.

“He will persuade you somehow and carry it out.”

I knew it! Geom Mugeuk’s shoulders drooped as he let out a sigh.

“Ah! If they were going to unify murim, they should have done it ages ago. Why did it have to come down to Father’s generation and make things difficult for me?”

“Do you know why our cult has never been able to unify murim until now?”

“Why not?”

“Because killing the Murim Alliance Leader and the martial artists belonging to the Murim Alliance does not end it. Their numbers are only a very small portion of the whole, and throughout the rivers and lakes, there are as many martial artists as grains of sand on a beach. Among them, orthodox martial artists are overwhelmingly numerous. If a Great Demon-Orthodox War breaks out, countless hidden orthodox masters will come pouring out. Do you think the orthodox sects are called strong in times of crisis for nothing?”

And was that all? Even if they won the war and took control of the Central Plains, endless resistance would follow. Ambushes targeting demonic practitioners would continue, and many of them would lose their lives.

It was a different situation from before his regression, when an overwhelming existence named Hwa Mugi had appeared and forced the demonic, orthodox, and unorthodox worlds to shut their gates. Between the three paths, resentment and anger toward one another had accumulated over a long time.

“Moreover, the unorthodox factions will never side with our cult. There is no way they would not know that after the orthodox factions, their turn would be next. In the end, it would become a war against an alliance of the orthodox and unorthodox paths.”

“Please say exactly that to Father.”

“Do you think the Cult Leader doesn’t know?”

Yes, he was right. His father would know better than anyone.

“I thought he would give it up because of me.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon lifted his head and stared at Geom Mugeuk. The words that came from his mouth were the exact opposite.

“He cannot give it up because of you.”

“Pardon?”

“I said he will push even harder because of you.”

At the unexpected words, Geom Mugeuk looked startled.

“What do you mean? Because of me? Why?”

“Stop thinking only from your own perspective and change the way you look at it. Think from the Cult Leader’s standpoint.”

Think from Father’s standpoint?

“If he persuades only you, he can wage the Great Demon-Orthodox War with you at his side. If you help, that war will become many times easier. Wouldn’t that war be tempting precisely because of you?”

Geom Mugeuk could not answer. He had never once thought about it from that perspective. Yet when he considered his own martial arts as they were now, it was not wrong.

“It isn’t merely because you are strong. If you are there, he will achieve victory while reducing the number of sacrifices by far. That is something you would desperately want. With that clever mind of yours, would you not conduct the war while minimizing the damage?”

He had never thought about that aspect either.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon understood his father perfectly.

“If I had a son like you, even I would want to try it once. Because with you, it would feel as though there was nothing I couldn’t accomplish.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk stared wordlessly at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, then chose to escape reality.

“I’m going to pretend I never heard what you said today. Father has abandoned his dream of unifying murim!”

“If that eases your heart.”

“I shouldn’t have asked. This is exactly why I was going to bring this up only after I’d had my fill of playing.”

Geom Mugeuk tucked the paper on the desk into his robe and weakly bade farewell.

“I’ll be going now.”

“Where are you going at this hour? To the masked fellow?”

“No. At least for today, I want to see someone who has absolutely nothing to do with our cult or the unification of murim.”

“And who is that?”

Opening the door and stepping out, Geom Mugeuk said,

“There is someone. A person who dreams of becoming the tavern owner who fights best in the world.”

* * *

The Sword King was sitting on a rock in the courtyard of his residence, looking up at the moon.

Geom Mugeuk entered that place.

“What are you doing alone so forlornly? You could at least have a drink.”

“I don’t like alcohol that much.”

Geom Mugeuk sat down beside him.

“A man who doesn’t even like alcohol wants to run a tavern?”

Earlier, when he had heard Cult Leader Pungcheon speak, he had realized that the Sword King wanted to open a tavern.

“If someone who likes alcohol opens a tavern, it’ll go under. Either the business will fail, or his health will.”

“All right, I’ll admit that. But what about a tavern owner being good at fighting? The better he is, the less suited it seems, doesn’t it?”

As he listened, the Sword King suddenly said,

“Speak comfortably with me from now on.”

“Pardon?”

“You said we were friends.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Sword King in surprise. The Sword King was trying to change their relationship.

“May I really do that?”

The Sword King nodded. Geom Mugeuk had killed even the Heavenly Killing Star and had given him a completely new life. Even if Geom Mugeuk told him to treat him as his elder brother, he was of a mind to say, Yes, I will.

“Brother Ak, I’ll speak casually from now on. It feels like we’ve really become friends now.”

If the other person had not been Geom Mugeuk, he would not have told him to drop formalities, and if the other person had not been the Sword King, Geom Mugeuk would not have done so.

“But are you really going to open a tavern? It doesn’t sound like something you just said in passing.”

“I want to try living a different life.”

“Why a tavern, of all things?”

“Because I don’t think it would be boring. I could hear what’s happening in the world through the mouths of the customers.”

Geom Mugeuk imagined the tavern the Sword King would open. Ak Gunhak with his ponytail and bare feet—at first thought, it did seem as if it would suit him better than anyone.

“A retired absolute master opening a tavern and living normally? It is stylish.”

“But?”

“Won’t you get tired of that ordinary life right away? I think I’d get sick of smelling food after just a month. And you’d have to deal with all sorts of awful customers. With that temper of yours, could you endure it?”

The Sword King could not answer no. Right now, it was not that he absolutely had to do it; he merely felt, Shall I try it?

“If I try it and it’s not fun, I can quit.”

Geom Mugeuk stared quietly at the Sword King, then offered unexpected advice.

“Instead of that, why not do it properly from the start?”

“Properly? How?”

“Build it on the scale of the largest inn in the Central Plains. You’ve earned that much money, haven’t you?”

To a man who said he would try it and quit if it did not suit him, he was telling him to build the largest inn in the Central Plains?

“Build it as a top-class establishment and charge high prices. Sell fine cuisine, and make the guest rooms the best-equipped. Train the waiters to be courteous and set them apart from other places. Make it the kind of inn people say is the best in the Central Plains, the kind they want to visit at least once if they make money. And the most important thing is this.”

It was a rule that had never been seen in an inn before.

“When people enter this inn, they must leave all their weapons at the entrance. And set a rule that no fighting is permitted inside. All martial artists of the demonic, orthodox, and unorthodox paths may enter, but they must never fight. But if they break that rule? Brother Ak, you have something you’re good at, don’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk drew the edge of his hand across his neck.

“You would be establishing the safest inn in all of murim.”

The Sword King’s expression turned slightly interested.

“If word spreads, all sorts of bastards will swarm in.”

“That’s the fun of it. Wouldn’t every day be interesting?”

There was a reason Geom Mugeuk was saying this to him.

“Brother Ak, with those martial arts, it’s hard to live ordinarily. Since you only live once anyway, live in a way that’s magnificent and fun. Brother Ak, you’re someone who wants to leave suddenly for somewhere far away, aren’t you? I think the more someone is like that, the more they need to live loudly like this.”

In any case, his was not a life where he could simply leave for some place where no one knew him. He had promised the Demon Cult Leader that he would protect Geom Mugeuk. No matter where he lived or what he did, if Geom Mugeuk needed help, he would come running to assist him.

“And don’t think of doing it alone. Do it together with Squad Leader Cha. Brother Ak, you have a surprisingly soft side, so if you do it alone, you’ll only end up taking losses all the time.”

At the mention of Cha Iran, the Sword King flinched.

“You don’t dislike Squad Leader Cha either, do you? If rumors spread that even a peerless beauty like Squad Leader Cha is at the inn, you’ll probably make a fortune. You’ll become a great tycoon through an inn.”

The Sword King burst out laughing. It was truly advice befitting Geom Mugeuk. The problem was that this absurd advice was tempting. Even in this battle, there had been moments when he had prepared himself for death. With a life like that, what could he not do?

Just then, Cult Leader Pungcheon walked in and said,

“The name of the inn has been decided. How about Hero Inn?”

Originally, he had prepared a different name, but while coming over and hearing Geom Mugeuk’s words, he had changed his mind.

“I like it. An inn run by the greatest swordsman under heaven and the greatest beauty assassin under heaven is called Hero Inn.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and added,

“If you’re a martial artist, could you possibly resist?”

Chapter 845: Even the Fearsome Heavenly Demon Soul Smiles
---------------------------------------------------------
While Geom Mugeuk went to fetch the wine, Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Sword King sat side by side, looking up at the moon.

After the fight at Pungryu Tavern, they had grown rather close, so being alone together did not feel awkward at all.

“Hero Inn… what a magnificent name.”

At the Sword King’s words, Cult Leader Pungcheon wore a proud expression. In truth, it was a name that had come to him the moment he heard Geom Mugeuk speak upon entering the place.

“I gave it much thought for your sake.”

“Thank you. But I’m not sure whether I can live such a life.”

Geom Mugeuk’s proposal was not something one could accept without an ordinary amount of resolve.

“I’ve lived only for myself. Could I live while looking after others?”

If he ran an inn on a large scale, the number of workers alone would reach several dozen. There were the people who would work at the inn, of course, but would he really be able to maintain a good relationship with Cha Iran as well?

No, before that, would Cha Iran even want to live such a life with him?

Even so, the fact that he was agonizing over it meant that some part of him did want to try running an inn like that. All the more so because he had lived his life thinking only of leaving for somewhere else.

“If a man sets his mind to it, what in this world can he not do? The hard part is that damned setting of the mind.”

The Sword King knew why Cult Leader Pungcheon said such a thing. After all, as far as he knew, the person who had made the greatest decision and changed his life the most was none other than Cult Leader Pungcheon.

If it had been him, could he have thrown away the position of cult leader and come away? Even opening a single inn had him hesitating this much.

“Cult Leader, do you not regret the decision you made?”

Many emotions crossed Cult Leader Pungcheon’s face. It was something he could have simply laughed off without answering, but he revealed his honest feelings.

“Why would I not?”

Especially difficult had been the period when he wandered the Central Plains with Gowol, establishing Silver Moon’s branches and sub-branches. Watching over Gowol, who was utterly immersed in the work, had meaning, but creating Silver Moon had not been something he himself had wanted.

“It is shameful to say, but there were nights when I fell asleep thinking of the days when I lived as Cult Leader Pungcheon. I missed that wretched sandstorm.”

The Sword King nodded with an expression that said he understood what he meant.

Cult Leader Pungcheon was honest with the Sword King.

Even without the added merit of thinking that a man Geom Mugeuk liked this much must surely be a decent person, the Sword King he had come to know during this time possessed a certain charm.

He was the sort of man who inspired trust that, at the very least, he would not use an openhearted confession as a weakness.

“And now? Do you still regret it?”

“Now… I do from time to time.”

At those words, the Sword King laughed, and Cult Leader Pungcheon laughed along with him.

“This is all because of the Young Cult Leader. So you should be careful too. Whenever you lie down to sleep at that inn, you may long for this moment. There is no law saying you cannot become ‘that horsetail-haired fellow from back then.’ Ah! I must have been bewitched then. Why was I bewitched by that Young Cult Leader who has not even once come to visit the inn?”

Then a voice came from behind them.

“As expected! Only now do you sound like our cult leader.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had gone to get the wine, had just arrived.

“Are you not the sort of man who hides and waits until I curse you before appearing?”

“I can see why you would misunderstand!”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and sat between the two of them.

“On a day like this, we should have a drink. It is a meaningful day when our Brother Ak and I have begun speaking comfortably, and also the day we gained the splendid name Hero Inn.”

“I have not decided yet.”

The Sword King took a step back, but in Geom Mugeuk’s mind, the inn had already opened its doors.

“What are you so worried about? Later, if things become troublesome and the situation gets complicated, write this in big letters and put it up at the entrance: The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult is my friend. Ju Inbaek.”

The Sword King laughed and shook his head.

“Then that troublesome situation would become ten times bigger.”

“And it would be ten times more entertaining for us watching.”

Geom Mugeuk had even brought cups with meticulous care.

“Now, please accept a cup from me.”

He poured wine for Cult Leader Pungcheon first, then poured for the Sword King as well.

“Take a cup from me.”

The Sword King poured wine for Geom Mugeuk.

They raised their cups in a toast and drank. Sitting on the broad rock in the courtyard, drinking with moonlight as their side dish—

“This is good.”

“It is.”

The words came naturally from all of their mouths.

As he set down his cup, Geom Mugeuk let out the words that had been deep in his heart.

“I am glad everyone is safe.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk. They knew well how hard he had struggled to save them all.

Cult Leader Pungcheon said to Geom Mugeuk,

“You saved them all.”

A smile formed at Geom Mugeuk’s lips. He was always the one who said what others wanted to hear, but how could he himself not want to hear such words?

He had truly wanted to hear them.

Well done. You saved them all.

He had worked through those long years to hear that single sentence.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Cult Leader Pungcheon with gentle eyes.

“And you saved me, Cult Leader. Thanks to you, I am sitting here drinking wine.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon liked taking credit, but at least when it came to that final battle, he did not.

“I merely thought it was an opportunity and stepped forward first to claim the credit. Who among those there wanted you to die? Even if not killing him meant everyone would die… as long as you were inside that man’s body, your father would not have killed him. I am certain of it.”

Geom Mugeuk suddenly remembered what the old man had said. The people you made splendid will lead you to become even more splendid.

“I heard you stood in front of me and said to my father, ‘Have you gone mad?’”

Cult Leader Pungcheon waved his hands.

“I was the one who had gone mad then.”

At those words, they all laughed together.

The three of them drank in good spirits.

After they had emptied all the wine and were rising to leave, Geom Mugeuk promised a next time.

“When Ian and Squad Leader Cha return, the four of you should have a drink.”

“All right.”

At the mention of Cha Iran, the Sword King shrank back slightly. Geom Mugeuk did not miss the change in his expression.

“That is why Brother Ak absolutely needs Squad Leader Cha!”

Geom Mugeuk and Cult Leader Pungcheon left the Sword King’s residence.

As they walked together through the inner grounds, Cult Leader Pungcheon briefly stopped.

“Young Cult Leader.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

“May I ask you something?”

“Please do.”

An unexpected worry flowed from his mouth.

“Is my being beside Gowol a burden to him?”

“A burden?”

“I mean it as I said. Because he brought me here, might he not feel that he has to take responsibility for me until the end?”

“You freely curse the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, yet you still mind Strategist Go’s feelings.”

As Cult Leader Pungcheon smiled awkwardly, Geom Mugeuk gave him strength.

“You persuaded him like that back then and brought him here, so how could he find you burdensome? If he does, just leave at once. People only realize someone is precious after they are gone.”

“He is so busy that he might not even notice I disappeared.”

Once one began worrying over someone, needless imaginings could sometimes devour the entire relationship. At times like this, the best thing was—

“Since we are on the subject, shall we go see Strategist Go? He probably has not gone to sleep yet, has he?”

“He will be working.”

“Let us go. We can at least see his face before we leave.”

Geom Mugeuk turned his steps toward Silver Moon with Cult Leader Pungcheon. When they arrived, he asked the martial artist guarding the entrance to call Gowol.

As they waited, Geom Mugeuk suddenly spoke. It was his answer to the question Cult Leader Pungcheon had asked earlier.

“I think the opposite.”

“What do you mean?”

“Strategist Go is probably worrying like this: What if the Cult Leader finds me burdensome? What if he is needlessly tied down by me and missing the chance to roam freely across the Central Plains? What if, at this rate, the Cult Leader loses interest in someone like me? Even if he does not show it, he must be terribly concerned.”

“Do you really think so?”

“If it were me, I would be.”

Geom Mugeuk’s firm answer improved Cult Leader Pungcheon’s mood. Even though he knew the words were meant to comfort him, they gave him strength.

Just then, Gowol ran out of the building with a delighted face.

“Young Cult Leader, congratulations on repelling the great enemy.”

Gowol respectfully cupped his fist and offered a formal greeting.

“It was thanks to you guarding the rear.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon needlessly acted peevish.

“And me! I fought with my life on the line too.”

Gowol looked at Cult Leader Pungcheon.

“You worked hard as well, Cult Leader.”

His words were courteous, but there was a hint of mischief in his eyes.

If not for the Young Cult Leader, his eyes seemed to say, Cult Leader! You worked even harder! Seeing that gaze, Cult Leader Pungcheon thought that he had worried for nothing. Gowol was busy with work, yet he remained unchanged like this.

Gowol asked Geom Mugeuk,

“How do you feel?”

“Refreshed, yet empty.”

Not even a day had passed since he had killed Hwa Mugi.

Gowol was reading Geom Mugeuk’s heart precisely.

“Are you more worried about what lies ahead?”

“You also think Father intends to unify the murim.”

As expected, Gowol was of the same mind as the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Yes. The Cult Leader will certainly try to achieve the unification of the murim.”

If even Gowol saw it that way, then at the very least, that meant it was reality.

“If you were to offer advice as my strategist?”

Even if the Demon Venerables did not want it, they were people who had to obey if it was his father’s command. The Wicked Smiling Demon or the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable might follow him, but the remaining Demon Venerables would all follow his father.

But Gowol was not yet the strategist of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. He was Geom Mugeuk’s strategist.

“There is one surest way to stop the unification of the murim. However, it is a method you will not want, Young Cult Leader.”

“Tell me.”

It was a method he had not thought of, and just as Gowol said, it was also one he did not want.

“You must become the Cult Leader before the promised five years are up.”

“!”

But at the same time, it was also the surest method. After all, only the Cult Leader could start a war.

The problem was that with his father still hale and strong, he had no desire whatsoever to ascend to the position of cult leader.

“Is there a way to become Cult Leader?”

Gowol did not answer. His expression said he knew, but could never speak it aloud.

From that reaction, Geom Mugeuk could guess the method.

To spar with his father and win.

If his son became stronger than him, his father would yield the position of Cult Leader to him.

Geom Mugeuk spoke calmly.

“As you know, I once served as temporary Cult Leader. I was stuck in the Heavenly Demon Hall all day, working. It was truly stifling and difficult. But I also came to realize that because Father endures that hardship, countless people of our cult are able to live.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze, which had been on Gowol, turned toward the moon.

“The day Father steps down, I will make it the most honorable day in this world. I will make it a day worthy of the effort and devotion he has given throughout his life, protecting the Heavenly Demon Hall.”

Gowol bowed his head respectfully. Since Gowol was intelligent, he would understand well what Geom Mugeuk was saying. He must have clearly understood Geom Mugeuk’s will that he would never defeat his father to ascend to the position of cult leader.

Cult Leader Pungcheon, who had been listening quietly, found another method.

“Listen, Young Cult Leader. I have thought of a way to stop the unification of the murim.”

With Geom Mugeuk and Gowol’s gazes focused on him, an unexpected method emerged.

“Get married and give him a grandson.”

Thinking it was a joke, Geom Mugeuk laughed. But Cult Leader Pungcheon was serious.

“I am not saying this in jest. If he holds in his arms a cute, handsome grandson who resembles you, his desire to wage war will vanish completely. No, wait, since he raised only sons, give him a granddaughter. Trust me! Even that fearsome Heavenly Demon Soul will smile before a granddaughter!”

Unlike Geom Mugeuk, who laughed it off as nonsense, Gowol was nodding seriously.

* * *

“You have not gone to sleep yet?”

When Geom Mugeuk went to see him, Geom Ujin was standing in the inner courtyard of his residence, hands clasped behind his back, looking up at the night sky.

“Did you not come because you thought that would be the case?”

“Yes, that is right.”

His father always went to sleep early, but somehow, Geom Mugeuk had felt that he would still be awake tonight. So he had used his internal energy to dispel the few cups of wine he had drunk and came with a clear mind.

Geom Mugeuk approached his father’s side. It was the same moon he had seen from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s room, the same moon he had seen with Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Sword King, and the same moon he had seen with Gowol. Now he looked up at that moon together with his father.

Before, he had looked at it without much thought, but as he looked up at the moon with his father, his heart swelled. Because he was standing with the person he had wanted most to save among all those he wished to save.

“How do you feel?”

“Good. So good that I do not think I will be able to sleep tonight. Ah, and please look at this.”

Geom Mugeuk took a piece of paper from his robe. It was the schedule he had written earlier in the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s room.

“There are this many things I have to do now. Not there—look here. Do not think of squeezing into the Four Handsome Men and making the Demon Venerables uncomfortable.”

The corners of Geom Ujin’s mouth rose. It was the first welcome sneer from his father that he had seen since Hwa Mugi’s death.

“Here it is. Travel with Father! I want to go on another journey with you, Father!”

He had naturally expected his father to say he was busy.

“Prepare the fishing rods then too.”

His father was waiting for a rematch of their fishing contest.

And tonight, he had been waiting for his son.

“Use the Space-Time Transposition Art.”

Geom Mugeuk did as he was told and used the Space-Time Transposition Art.

“That as well.”

Geom Mugeuk obediently used the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven as well, then made it so that the outside scenery could be seen.

Though he had experienced the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven once before, Geom Ujin was still amazed when he saw it again. Time was flowing so slowly it seemed to have stopped. He could see with his eyes the minute fluttering of an insect’s wings as it flew toward the lantern hanging on the wall.

‘I may not know about anything else, but I will not teach you the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven. I am sorry, Father.’

But his father had not told him to open this place in order to ask him to teach him the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven.

“Your Gu Hwa Demonic Art rose by one level.”

“Yes. This battle helped.”

His Gu Hwa Demonic Art had now reached the eleventh star. There was now only one step left until the perfected twelfth star. Of course, if fortune was poor, that one step could be one he failed to take even after an entire lifetime.

Geom Ujin drew the Heavenly Demon Sword and said,

“You will not leave until you achieve the perfected twelfth star.”

Geom Mugeuk was startled. The reason his father had made him use the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven was to train him. The reason he wore his sword even late at night was also because he had kept in mind that his son would come.

His father surely knew that if his son became even stronger than he was now, it would become more difficult for him to achieve his own dream.

And yet instead of asking to be taught the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven, his father was trying to help his son achieve the perfected twelfth star. Because he was this kind of father.

This is why this fight is the hardest for me.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said to his father,

“As you saw, I have many things to do…”

Geom Mugeuk drew the Black Demon Sword and said powerfully,

“I will leave before morning comes.”

Chapter 846: I Want to Achieve It Slowly
----------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk displayed all of his strength in the confrontation with his father.

No, he had to do it even if he didn't want to.

Swaeeeeeak!

His father's attacks flying toward him were, without exaggeration, fiercer than a blade of fire.

'Father, it's me, your son! Not an enemy, not the Thousand Kills Star!'

He didn't even have the luxury to spout such nonsense. The moment he lost focus was the moment he died.

Geom Woojin had returned to being that ruthless father who had whipped wind across his son's face when they first climbed the mountain.

Considering the purpose of this training, these lethal attacks were only natural.

And this confrontation was a training that required an extraordinary mindset beyond mere skill.

It was a fight where lethal techniques ran rampant, yet he absolutely must not harm his opponent. He had to display extreme focus and judgment.

It was a confrontation that only Geom Woojin and Geom Mugeuk could have carried out from the very beginning.

In the exchange between father and son, right answers and wrong answers repeated.

'This is the answer,' said his father's flying attack, and Geom Mugeuk blocked it, saying, 'Wrong answer.'

Again, thinking, 'Then this must be the answer,' as the attack flew toward him, Geom Mugeuk desperately rendered it an incorrect answer.

Of course, that was only between the two of them; to others, every single one of those moves was an attack with the correct answer.

Geom Mugeuk desperately dodged his father's attacks.

He wouldn't die from attacks of this level. If he failed to meet the standard his father had set.

'He'll really kill me!'

When the first confrontation since entering the Space-Time Conversion Art ended like that, Geom Woojin recognized the change in his son.

"It wasn't just internal energy that you gained!"

His son's skill had clearly risen. Putting aside the fact that the Nine Transformations Demonic Art had risen from Ten Stars to Eleven Stars, his depth regarding the martial arts as a whole and his martial prowess had improved.

"I obtained all of their innate true qi and internal energy, of course, but even the martial arts they had mastered."

It was something impossible to achieve unless it were a gift sent from the heavens. After all, the realms they had spent their lifetimes reaching were given to him as they were.

Moreover, Geom Mugeuk could use those martial arts.

Originally, a person who mastered demonic arts could not use orthodox or unorthodox martial arts. Because the fundamental nature of the internal energy that served as the basis was different from the start.

But this time, as his innate true qi and internal energy combined, he could use all the martial arts he had obtained.

It meant that when he went out into the martial world in the future, he could appear like an orthodox practitioner, or pose as an unorthodox one.

Geom Woojin pointed his sword again. Just how many times would he have to say this before they could leave?

"Now, again!"

The two charged at each other.

Shwiiiiiiik.

Their movements were as fast as light, yet the insects of the outside world were still flying very slowly, having not yet reached the lamp.

* * *

"Am I still not fully sober?"

Chwima, looking out the window, blinked his eyes in surprise.

"Didn't you say we were entering our Maga Village just a moment ago?"

To Chwima's question, the warrior in the coachman's seat answered.

"That's correct, this is Maga Village."

The coachman was no less surprised. His voice trembled as he looked around in bewilderment.

"Halt the carriage."

As Chwima's carriage stopped, the carriages following behind also came to a halt.

When Chwima got out of the carriage, Mabul and the Soul-Seizing Demon Lord also got down. They were equally surprised.

Maga Village had disappeared without a trace.

The three Demon Lords exchanged glances. They had already heard the urgent message from the Tongcheon Pavilion that the Thousand Kills Star had been repelled.

But that fight had been this fierce?

The large carriage carrying the Ghost Shadow Squad and Magun that had ridden up behind them also stopped.

The most surprised among them was Ian. Because she knew better than anyone how important this place was to Geom Mugeuk.

'Young Cult Leader!'

In the place where Maga Village had disappeared, demonic followers and workers were preparing for construction. They were dividing the area and discussing where to erect which buildings.

The demonic followers and workers recognized the Demon Lords and politely bowed their heads in greeting.

One person walked out from among them. He was none other than the Eldest Young Master, Geom Muyang. The Chief Strategist, Sima Ming, had appointed him as the person in charge of the reconstruction of Maga Village. Because of his meticulous personality, he was better at handling such practical matters than Geom Mugeuk.

"Eldest Young Master!"

"Lord Mabul!"

Geom Muyang exchanged greetings with the three Demon Lords. Ian and Jang Ho also came to greet him.

Geom Muyang first examined the Demon Lords' injuries.

"Are your injuries alright?"

"We are all fine."

"That's a relief."

Mabul asked with a worried gaze.

"What happened?"

"As you may have heard, the Cult Leader repelled the Thousand Kills Star here."

Hearing the added remark that the Cult Leader and the residents of Maga Village were all safe, Ian asked in a trembling voice.

"Is the Young Cult Leader unharmed?"

While waiting for the answer, Ian's heart beat as if it would burst. If a situation where Maga Village was blown away entirely had occurred, Geom Mugeuk would have tried to stop it by any means. She had worried that he might have been injured in the process.

Fortunately, that had not happened.

"The Young Cult Leader is also safe."

"Ah!"

Just as Ian let out a sigh of relief, startling news was delivered to the Demon Lords.

"Father and Mugeuk have entered into joint closed-door training."

Everyone was startled at the words "joint closed-door training." They had never heard of the Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader entering closed-door training together until now.

"It is the second day since they entered closed-door training."

Mabul cautiously asked.

"Don't tell me...?"

Mabul worried that the two had entered joint closed-door training to hide from outsiders that they were injured and receiving treatment.

Geom Muyang, reading Mabul's worry, smiled.

"There is no need for concern."

Only then did Mabul and everyone else feel relief at Geom Muyang's bright expression.

"You must be tired from the long journey, so please go and rest."

Mabul said as he climbed into the carriage.

"Now that it's the second day, the Cult will be quiet for a while!"

Chwima climbed into the carriage and urged the coachman on.

"Let's hurry. I can smell the liquor stored in the Drunken Dream Tower from here."

The Soul-Seizing Demon Lord said nothing, but he wanted to see the Young Cult Leader more than ever. Because he had safely finished the battle as the Young Cult Leader's first Demon Lord.

The carriages carrying the Demon Lords sped toward the main hall.

Ian stood there for a moment, gazing at the place where the Pleasure Tavern had been.

A tinge of regret flitted across her face.

That the Cult Leader and Geom Mugeuk were safe was an immeasurably fortunate thing, but it was heartbreaking that Maga Village had been blown away.

The Pleasure Tavern in particular was a place of memories for her.

The moment she drank with Geom Mugeuk came to mind. She had been so drunk that he carried her on his back. The moments she had drinking gatherings with Seo Daeryong and Jang Ho also came to mind.

It had felt truly good to drink at the place where the Three-Party Talks were achieved.

She felt regret that she should have come more often. Lately, she had been too busy, and since Seo Daeryong had even gotten married, they hadn't been able to have drinking gatherings.

Then Jang Ho came and stood beside her.

"Leader Yi, do not take it too hard."

"Lord Jang, once it's rebuilt here, let's resume our drinking gatherings."

"Very well."

After promising their next meeting, the large carriages led by Jang Ho also left that place.

Ian was about to tell her subordinates that they should get going too, when she saw Chairan staring somewhere.

Ian walked over to Chairan's side.

"What are you looking at?"

"I'm looking at the scene that makes me out to be a cruel leader who obstructs love."

What did she mean? Ian turned her gaze to where Chairan was looking.

The person Chairan was looking at was Sipchilgyo.

Sipchilgyo was looking at one place, and among the workers, there was a young man. The gazes they exchanged were deeply affectionate.

The man was none other than the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest. He was the one who had previously removed his mask and shown his face to her.

Hearing the news that she had nearly arrived, he had been waiting among the workers. Since only a few people in the immediate vicinity knew of their relationship, they were meeting secretly like this.

"They're really flaunting their affection."

At her words, Ian laughed and said,

"Even during war, children are born and smoke rises from cooking stoves, no?"

Chairan said to Ian,

"Why don't you flaunt some affection too, Leader?"

"Look who's talking?"

The two women smiled at each other.

"It's really over now."

Ian added as she looked around.

"Completely clean."

Thinking of the forces behind this incident, Geom Mugeuk was the first to come to mind for Ian. After he left the cult, she had been so worried that she focused solely on martial arts training. Thanks to that, one could say she had grown strong enough to reach her current level.

Ian looked at Chairan.

"How do you feel?"

The fact that this fight had ended would hold great meaning for her as well, just as it did for herself.

"A relief."

She felt as though she had cast off shackles she had worn for a long time. The darkness that had been connected to her was completely severed. Now was the moment a new life began.

Chairan said to her subordinates.

"Alright, disperse here. Get some good rest today!"

They dispersed here for Sipchilgyo's sake. It was her consideration, letting them stay together a bit longer since she was so overjoyed.

"What do you say the section leaders have a drink together later?"

Ian nodded at Chairan's words. If life was something where everything could disappear overnight like this.

"Sounds good."

She was determined not to put it off anymore.

* * *

Two days had already passed since entering the Space-Time Conversion Art.

Since the time outside was two days, quite a long time had passed inside.

The bold declaration that they would leave before morning arrived had crumbled before the difficulty of achieving the Twelve-Star Grand Success. Right, as if such a thing were easy.

The training continued. On some days they clashed with the Nine Transformations Demonic Art; on others, with the Flying Heaven Sword Art; and on still others, they sparred with the most basic sword techniques.

Whenever they grew accustomed to each other's swordsmanship, his father unfolded a powerful technique without fail. For that moment alone, it was a terrifying attack that made him think, 'I'm going to die like this!'

Geom Mugeuk, who had narrowly survived death again today, announced mealtime.

"Father, it's time to eat."

"Yes, let's eat."

Snap!

When Geom Mugeuk snapped his fingers, a table was placed on a field with a view of a beautiful lakeside.

Geom Mugeuk created various beautiful places for his father every mealtime.

"Don't waste your internal energy needlessly!"

"I cannot do that. Even if I unleash one technique less, you must eat your meals properly."

Geom Mugeuk took several of the foods stacked to one side and set the table.

The person responsible for providing the food from outside was Hwi.

Earlier, Geom Mugeuk had briefly come out, gathered a load of provisions, and asked Hwi to keep bringing food to the front of the area where the Space-Time Conversion Art was unfolded.

He could have taken several months' worth of meals in at once, but then his father would have had to eat only jerky or dried foods that could be kept for a long time.

So Geom Mugeuk had the chefs of the Heavenly Demon Hall keep bringing food to this place.

He had told Hwi honestly. In the currently unfolded space, time flowed differently from the outside, so a lot of food was needed.

For two days, Hwi had left the food there every time the Heavenly Demon Hall chefs prepared a dish. The food disappeared as soon as it was left there.

"Today's cuisine is truly delicious. The chef is properly displaying his skills."

"That person must be having a hard time."

"What can we do? He'll have to suffer for a few days."

"Can we leave within a few days?"

"No, how did you achieve something this difficult, Father?"

What Geom Mugeuk had worried about for his father was not just the food.

He made time pass inside this Space-Time Conversion Art just like the outside as well.

The sun rose and set, night came and dawn broke.

This too was for his father. While his young self could get by day or night in a daze without issue, if his father lived like that for an extended period, it would cause health problems.

When it was time to sleep, he let him sleep, and he matched mealtimes exactly.

Creating an almost exact day inside this place was something impossible unless one was an absolute master like Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Woojin was inwardly moved by his son caring for him so much, but he did not show it outwardly.

After finishing the meal like this.

Snap!

Geom Mugeuk turned the surroundings into a walking path. Some days it became a beautiful bamboo forest, and other days it became the mountain trail they had seen at Daecheon Mountain.

On still other days, it became a snow-covered path that no one had walked. A path that had been so lonely when walking alone, but now he walked it with his father.

Geom Mugeuk cherished this time with his father so much. Truthfully, he didn't mind if he never achieved the Twelve-Star Grand Success of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art. This time he was spending with his father was simply too precious.

Geom Mugeuk knew better than anyone else how quickly this time, which seemed like it would remain forever, would pass in an instant.

He also knew that one could only realize how brilliant that moment was after it had passed.

After finishing the walk like that, they had evening training. In the evening, rather than real combat training, they mainly discussed martial arts.

"There is a technique called Dual Life Reversal among the Heavenly Will Palace Lord's martial arts. When unfolding this technique, the movement of internal force differs from other martial arts. In this technique, the internal force begins from the Zhongji acupoint..."

Geom Mugeuk hoped that this precious time with his father would not end with merely being taught by him.

Although his father had achieved Twelve-Star Grand Success, the path of martial arts was a path without end.

Because his father could reach yet another realm.

That was why he discussed with his father the various enlightenments he had gained while accepting the Martial King Divine Sword, the Wine God, and the Heavenly Will Palace Lord's martial arts.

Geom Mugeuk passed them on to his father without reserve.

And when it went to his father and returned, it came back containing an even deeper meaning of martial arts.

When even the evening training ended, Geom Mugeuk changed the Space-Time Conversion Art space into night.

And so that he could sleep in the most comfortable and familiar place, he recreated his father's room exactly.

Of course, he couldn't reproduce it perfectly, but he made it roughly similar with his excellent memory.

He hung the picture of him with his older brother as well, and didn't forget the dolls on top of the display shelf.

And he placed his own bed beside his father's large bed.

The few words exchanged with his father just before falling asleep were good too.

He asked about his father's time as the Young Cult Leader in the past, and about the Demon Lords as well. Mostly, he couldn't get an answer and just talked to himself.

But neither of them uttered a single word about the unification of the martial world. Neither father nor son.

"Let's sleep."

"Sleep well, Father. Thank you for today as well."

When Geom Mugeuk snapped his fingers, the surroundings grew dark.

"Tomorrow will be harder!"

Geom Mugeuk could tell. His father truly intended not to leave until he achieved Twelve-Star Grand Success.

'Please don't push too hard.'

Because this time with his father was too precious.

'I want to achieve it slowly, even if it takes one more day.'

Chapter 847: You Must Be Watching Me Even Now
---------------------------------------------
The training continued.

Geommugeuk was building up his efforts day by day. Fueled by the firm belief that he would undoubtedly achieve the Great Perfection of the Twelve Stars.

After all, the greatest was teaching the greatest to become the greatest.

Now, all that remained was effort.

He knew this from having experienced it many times before. Water continues to heat up until it boils over in one final, sudden instant. If you cannot endure that heat and stop, all that remains is cooling down.

But right now, it was too hot. To the point where he wanted to give up at any moment.

"Haa, haa."

Geommugeuk lay on the floor, gasping for breath.

Geomujin stood beside him with his hands clasped behind his back, gazing into the distance.

The intensity of the training rose by the day. To the point where his father, who was instructing him, was actually beginning to worry.

Plip, plop.

Then, raindrops began to fall one by one.

Whoosh.

Soon, a downpour began.

"Ah! It's raining. What sort of rain is this, so sudden!"

How brazenly innocent of a reaction, when he was the one who made it rain?

"Father, shall we take shelter over there for a moment?"

Before they knew it, a small pavilion had appeared behind them.

Geomujin gave a faint smile and walked toward it. An invisible energy guarded his body, so not a single drop of rain could wet him.

Whoosh.

The two sat side by side, admiring the falling rain.
